It’s not like I’m trying to be sloppy… it just feels harder to keep my mind on one thing there.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, did you have trouble focusing or staying on task when you were a child, like in school or while doing homework?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, to be honest. I mean, school was a long time ago. I don’t remember it being a big problem, or at least nobody made a big deal of it that I can recall. I did my work… maybe not perfectly, but I got through. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: As a child, did you ever have a hard time sitting still, waiting your turn, or staying focused on tasks like homework or chores?\n\nPatient: I don’t really remember having trouble like that, no. I mean, I’m sure I had normal kid moments, but I don’t recall teachers or my parents saying I couldn’t sit still or wait my turn. Homework and chores… I did them well enough, I think. I don’t want to say for sure if I’m forgetting something, but nothing stands out to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems with staying focused or getting distracted in other parts of your life, like while driving, talking with friends, or doing chores at home?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s at work, honestly. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but that’s what brought me in — at the warehouse, if a beeper goes off or there’s a forklift alarm, or even someone moving a pallet nearby, my mind just gets pulled off what I’m counting. Then I lose my place and later realize I entered the wrong inventory numbers.\n\nAt home or with friends, I guess I don’t notice it as much. It’s mainly when I’m trying to keep track of numbers and there’s a lot going on around me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed any difficulty focusing or getting distracted while driving, watching TV, or having conversations, even when there aren’t beeping noises or distractions like at work?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I notice it much with driving or TV or talking to people… not in the same way, anyway. What’s really been bothering me is at the warehouse. If a beeper goes off, or a forklift alarm, or even someone moving a pallet nearby, I get pulled off what I’m counting, and then later I realize I put the wrong numbers into inventory. I hope that makes sense — it’s mostly that kind of situation.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself struggling to stay focused or easily distracted in situations that require sustained attention, like reading, watching a movie, or following a conversation, even when there aren’t any loud noises or interruptions?\n\nPatient: I guess… not really with reading or movies or conversations, at least not that I’ve noticed in a big way. What keeps happening, and what made me worried enough to come in, is more at the warehouse. I’ll be counting inventory, and then a beeper goes off or a forklift alarm, or someone moves a pallet nearby, and my mind just gets pulled away. Then I lose the count and later see I put the wrong numbers in. \n\nI don’t know if that’s the kind of focus problem you mean, but that’s the main one I’m noticing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Then I’ll look back and realize I put the wrong numbers into inventory. I just thought I should get it checked out, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to focus or get distracted easily in other parts of your life, like at home or with friends, or is this mainly happening at work?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly at work, I think. A beeper or forklift alarm goes off, or somebody moves a pallet near me, and my mind just jumps to that. Then I look back and realize I put the wrong numbers in. I’m not trying to be careless or anything… it’s just been harder to keep my mind on the one thing.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, do you find yourself getting distracted easily, like when you're watching TV, reading, or talking with someone?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I don’t notice it as much at home. I mean, maybe a little here and there, but the main place it’s been a problem is at the warehouse. It’s not like I’m trying to be sloppy… it just feels harder to keep my mind on one thing there.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, did you have trouble focusing or staying on task when you were a child, like in school or while doing homework?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, to be honest. I mean, school was a long time ago. I don’t remember it being a big problem, or at least nobody made a big deal of it that I can recall. I did my work… maybe not perfectly, but I got through. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: As a child, did you ever have a hard time sitting still, waiting your turn, or staying focused on tasks like homework or chores?\n\nPatient: I don’t really remember having trouble like that, no. I don’t want to say for sure if I’m forgetting something, but nothing stands out to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems with staying focused or getting distracted in other parts of your life, like while driving, talking with friends, or doing chores at home?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s at work, honestly. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
